\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}^{{\sf PCA}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{k}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=r+1}^{\min(p-r,n)}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{k}-\widehat{\lambda}_{i}}

5:

Compute, for
1\leq k\leq r
,

\displaystyle\widecheck{\lambda}_{k}:=\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{k}}{1+\widehat{\gamma}^{{\sf PCA}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{k})}.

6:

Set

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{s_{\bm{e}_{i},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\big)^{2}:=\sum_{k\leq r,k\neq j}\frac{\widecheck{\lambda}_{k}\widecheck{\lambda}_{j}(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{k}(i))(1+\widehat{b_{k}^{{\sf PCA}}})}{n(\widecheck{\lambda}_{j}-\widecheck{\lambda}_{k})^{2}}+\frac{\widehat{\sigma}^{2}\widecheck{\lambda}_{j}\|\bm{\widehat{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{e}_{i}\|^{2}}{(\widecheck{\lambda}_{j}-\widehat{\sigma}^{2})}.

7:

Compute
{\sf C.I.}^{(\alpha)}(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}(i))
defined via

\displaystyle{\sf C.I.}^{(\alpha)}(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}(i)):=\bigg[\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}(i)\pm\Phi^{-1}\big(1-\alpha/2\big)\widehat{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{e}_{i},j}}\bigg]

8:

return
Estimated confidence interval
{\sf C.I.}^{(\alpha)}(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}(i))
.

Algorithm 4

Entrywise Confidence Intervals for
\bm{u}_{j}
– PCA

5
Previous Work

The analysis of spectral methods has a rich history, going back to
\ell_{2}
perturbation theory such as the Davis-Kahan Theorem
(
Davis and Kahan 1970
)
or Wedin’s Theorem
(
Wedin 1972
)
, and much of this theory now forms the basis of book-level treatments (e.g.
Stewart and Sun 1990
;
Kato 1995
). Classical deterministic matrix perturbation theory is remarkably effective in a number of statistical settings, and improvements on the deterministic theory have been obtained
(
Cai and Zhang 2018
;
Luo et al. 2021
;
Yu et al. 2015
;
Zhang 2022
)
, as well as improvements tailored explicitly for various types of random noise
(
O’Rourke et al. 2018
;
Koltchinskii and Xia 2016
;
Nadler 2008
)
. Notably, a number of works have studied the
\ell_{2}
perturbation in principal component analysis
(
Cai et al. 2021b
;
Cai et al. 2013
;
Koltchinskii and
Lounici 2017b
)
. Many of these works focus on providing bounds for estimated subspaces (or for eigenvectors with large eigengaps), though we single out
O’Rourke et al. 2023
, which provides optimal
\ell_{2}
perturbation bounds for individual eigenvectors in a matrix denoising context under similar eigengap conditions as in this work.

Beyond
\ell_{2}
perturbation theory, a number of works have also considered
\ell_{2,\infty}
perturbation theory, which quantifies the entrywise (or row-wise) fluctuations of eigenvectors (or subspaces). The works
Cape et al. 2019b
;
Fan et al. 2018
;
Eldridge et al. 2018
;
Damle and Sun 2020
;
Bhardwaj and Vu 2023
provide deterministic
\ell_{2,\infty}
bounds for the leading subspaces of low-rank matrices and apply their results to problems arising in high-dimensional statistics. Furthermore, by taking into account both the structural assumptions and the probabilistic nature of the noise, a number of works have also derived
\ell_{2,\infty}
perturbation bounds tailored to different probabilistic settings. For example,
Lei 2019
considers symmetric matrices with independent noise,
Cai et al. 2021a
and
Zhou and Chen 2023
study highly unbalanced matrices,
Agterberg and Sulam 2022
focus on matrices with additional sparse structure,
Agterberg and Zhang 2022
examine problems arising from tensor data analysis, and
Chen et al. 2019
;
Chen et al. 2022
study ranking problems, though this list is still incomplete. A general survey on applications of
\ell_{2,\infty}
perturbation theory in various statistical contexts can be found in
Chen et al. 2021b
.

The
\ell_{2,\infty}
subspace perturbation bounds considered in previous works are a special type of the more general linear functions studied in this work. The works
Koltchinskii and Xia 2016
and
Xia and Zhou 2019
study perturbation bounds for linear functions of eigenvectors, with the latter focusing on the “unbalanced” setting, wherein the column dimension is significantly larger than the row dimension. Similarly,
Koltchinskii and Lounici 2016
provides perturbation bounds for linear functions of principal components under Gaussian noise in a general Hilbert space. However, these previous results all operate in the “large eigengaps” regime, implicitly requiring that the eigengap
\Delta_{j}
is of similar order to the eigenvalue spike
\lambda_{r}
. The works
Cheng et al. 2021
;
Chen et al. 2021a
;
Li et al. 2022
all provide perturbation bounds for linear functions of eigenvectors in the “small eigengaps” regime, though both
Cheng et al. 2021
and
Chen et al. 2021a
require
asymmetric
noise, something that we do not consider herein.

This work is also closely related to a number of works studying the asymptotic theory for low-rank matrix models. For example,
Xia 2021
;
Xia 2019
;
Bao et al. 2021
consider asymptotic theory for the
\sin\Theta
distances in matrix denoising, and
Bao et al. 2022
;
Koltchinskii and
Lounici 2017b
study the asymptotic distribution of
\sin\Theta
distances for the spiked principal component analysis model. These works also pertain to the so-called “supercritical” regime in random matrix theory, for which there are a number of works studying the asymptotics for eigenvalues and eigenvectors in matrix denoising
(
Ding 2020
;
Capitaine 2018
;
Benaych-Georges and
Nadakuditi 2011
;
Benaych-Georges and Nadakuditi 2012
;
Knowles and Yin 2014
;
Capitaine et al. 2012
;
Capitaine et al. 2009
)

and PCA
(
Ding and Yang 2021
;
Paul 2007
;
Bloemendal et al. 2016
;
Baik et al. 2005
;
Ding 2021
;
Onatski 2012
;
Johnstone and Lu 2009
)
, though these lists are incomplete.

Turning to asymptotic distributional theory, the works
Koltchinskii and
Lounici 2017a
;
Ding and Yang 2021
;
Bao et al. 2022
study the asymptotics for bilinear forms of spectral projectors in spiked covariance models, and
Koltchinskii et al. 2020
study efficient estimation in these settings. In a matrix denoising setting,
Fan et al. 2020
study linear functions of eigenvectors of symmetric matrices when eigenvalues diverge. However, in the aforementioned works, the emphasis has primarily been on the “large eigengaps” regime, and the results typically do not hold for finite samples.

Finally, statistical inference in low-rank matrix models is a relatively nascent field, with only a few results in specific contexts
(
Agterberg et al. 2022
;
Yan et al. 2021
;
Carpentier et al. 2019
;
Silin and Fan 2020
;
Carpentier and Nickl 2015
;
Carpentier et al. 2018
;
Chernozhukov et al. 2023
;
Cai et al. 2023
;
Xia and Yuan 2021
)
. Considering estimating linear functionals,
Koltchinskii et al. 2020
show that it is possible to find asymptotically efficient confidence intervals, though their estimators still rely on sample splitting. Notably, the work
Cheng et al. 2021
provides inferential procedures for linear functions of eigenvectors under asymmetric, heteroskedastic noise.

6
Numerical Simulations

In this section we demonstrate our theory through simulations. Each result is generated through 200 Monte Carlo iterations.

6.1
Matrix Denoising

Figure 2

Empirical (dotted) and theoretical (solid) ellipses for the quantity

with varying signal-strength and eigengaps under the matrix denoising model (
1
). From top to bottom the eigengap increases, and from left to right the signal strength increases.

In this section we demonstrate our theoretical results for matrix denoising.

Setup:
In all simulations we set
n=200
and
\sigma^{2}=1
, and
r=3
. To generate the matrix
\bm{S}
, we first define a vector
\bm{v}
with entries consisting of
n
independent
U(-1,1)
random variables, and we define
\bm{u}_{1}=\frac{\bm{v}}{\|\bm{v}\|}
. We then draw
\bm{u}_{2}
by drawing a new vector
\bm{v}^{\prime}
with
U(-1,1)
entries and setting
\bm{u}_{2}=\big(\bm{I}-\bm{u}_{1}\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}\big)\frac{\bm{v}^{\prime}}{\|\bm{v}^{\prime}\|}
to ensure orthogonality. Finally we set
\bm{u}_{3}
by drawing another vector
\bm{v}^{\prime\prime}
with
U(-1,1)
entries and setting
\bm{u}_{3}=\big(\bm{I}-\bm{u}_{1}\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{u}_{2}\bm{u}_{2}^{\top}\big)\frac{\bm{v}}{\|\bm{v}\|}
. Finally, for the varying levels of signal-to-noise ratio
\lambda_{\min}/\sigma\equiv\lambda_{\min}
and eigengap
\Delta_{1}
, we define
\lambda_{1}=5\lambda_{\min}
,
\lambda_{2}=\lambda_{1}-\Delta_{1}
,
\lambda_{3}=\lambda_{\min}
. We first draw the matrix
\bm{S}
before running simulations, and we keep it fixed throughout all simulations. The matrix
\bm{N}
is re-drawn at each Monte Carlo iteration.

Distributional Theory:
Plotted in
Fig.

2
is the empirical histogram of the random variable
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{1}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{1}\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{1}
, with solid line corresponding to the theoretical distribution (mean zero and variance
s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},1}
) and dotted line corresponding to the empirical distribution, with
\bm{a}
equal to the constant vector with entries
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
. By
Theorem

3.1
the approximate Gaussianity is governed by the eigengap
\Delta_{1}/\sigma
and the signal strength
\lambda_{\min}/\sigma
. From left to right we consider increasing signal strength and from top to bottom we consider increasing eigengap, so that top left has the smallest eigengap and signal strength, and bottom right has the largest eigengap and signal strength. Furthermore, by
Theorem

3.1
, the asymptotic variance decreases with larger eigengap and signal strength, and observe that the theoretical and empirical histograms become narrower as these quantities increase. We also ran simulations for the difference
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{1}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{1}
, but the figures are not materially different.

Approximate Coverage Rates:
We also consider approximate coverage rates for
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{1}
using
Algorithm

1
as well as the individual entry
\bm{u}_{1}(1)
using
Algorithm

2
. Similar to the previous setting we examine increasing eigengaps and signal-strengths with
\alpha=.05
. The column denoted “Mean” corresponds to the number of times the true value of either
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{1}
or
\bm{u}_{1}(1)
was in the outputted confidence interval, and the column denoted “Std” denotes the standard deviation of this value. Due to the sign ambiguity, we multiplied
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{1}
and
\bm{u}_{1}(1)
by
{\sf sign}(\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{1})
, as we assume this quantity is positive throughout our theory. In both cases the coverage remains close to
.95
.

,

and

, and we draw

, and for a given eigengap
\Delta_{1}
and signal strength
\lambda_{\min}/\sigma^{2}\equiv\lambda_{\min}
, we set
\lambda_{1}=5\lambda_{\min}
,
\lambda_{2}=\lambda_{1}-\Delta_{1}
, and
\lambda_{3}=\lambda_{\min}
.

Distributional Theory:
Plotted in

is the empirical histogram of the random variable
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{1}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{1}\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{1}
, with solid line corresponding to the theoretical distribution (mean zero and variance
s_{\bm{a},1}^{{\sf PCA}}
), and dotted line corresponding to the empirical distribution with
\bm{a}
equal to the constant vector with entries
\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}
. From left to right we consider increasing signal strength and from top to bottom we consider increasing eigengap. Similar to matrix denoising, the Gaussian approximation improves and the theoretical variance decreases as the eigengap and signal strength increase, which we see demonstrated in the figure.

Approximate Coverage Rates:
We also consider approximate coverage rates for
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{1}
using
Algorithm

3
as well as the individual entry
\bm{u}_{1}(1)
using
Algorithm

4
. Similar to the matrix denoising we examine increasing eigengaps and signal-strengths with
\alpha=.05
. The column denoted “Mean” corresponds to the number of times the true value of either
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{1}
or
\bm{u}_{1}(1)
was in the outputted confidence interval, and the column denoted “Std” denotes the standard deviation of this value. Due to the sign ambiguity, we multiplied
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{1}
and
\bm{u}_{1}(1)
by
{\sf sign}(\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{1})
, as we assume this quantity is positive throughout our theory. Observe that empirically the coverage improves as the signal strength and eigengap increases.

7
Discussion

In this work we have considered estimating linear forms of the form
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}
for a pre-specified unit vector
\bm{a}
. We have seen how small eigengaps and signal strength affect the approximate Gaussianity of both the plugin and debiased estimators, and we have provided asymptotically valid and statistically efficient confidence intervals for
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}
. All of our results hold under nearly minimal signal-strength conditions, and our proposed procedures are fully data-driven and do not require any sample splitting.

There are a number of potential future works. First, our analysis relies heavily on the assumption of Gaussian noise, which may not hold in general. It is of interest to develop similar statistical theory under general noise mechanisms such as subgaussian or heteroskedastic noise. Furthermore, our results are likely suboptimal with respect to logarithmic terms, factors of
r
, and factors of the condition number
\kappa
, and it would be interesting to study the optimal dependence on these parameters. For example, in matrix denoising, our analysis requires that
\Delta_{j}/\sigma\gg\mathrm{polylog}(n)
assuming
r,\kappa=O(1)
.
Does asymptotic normality hold even when
\Delta_{j}\asymp\sigma
provided certain necessary signal-strength conditions are met?

Beyond these immediate future directions, it would also be of interest to study similar problems in other settings. For example, the work
Xia et al. 2022
studies statistical inference for linear functions of tensor singular vectors; it would be interesting to see if similar guarantees can be developed when the tensor singular value gaps are of similarly small order as considered herein. Finally, the work
Xia 2019
considers confidence regions for singular subspaces in low-rank trace regression; it would be of interest to develop similar guarantees for individual singular subspaces in the presence of small singular value gaps.

8
Technical Tools and Proofs of Main Results

In this section we prove our key main results; namely,
Theorems

3.1
,
3.2
,
3.3
,
3.4
,
4.1
and
4.2
. Our main results rely on two entirely deterministic expansions, which we discuss in the next section. We then prove the results for matrix denoising and for PCA in
Sections

8.2
and
8.3
respectively. Proofs of additional lemmas and corollaries can be found in the appendix.

8.1
Matrix Analytic Tools

The bulk of our analysis is built off the following deterministic error decomposition that yields the precise form of the leading-order term.

Theorem 8.1

(First-Order Expansion)
.

Let
\{\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j},\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\}
denote the eigenvectors and eigenvalues of the symmetric matrix
\bm{\widehat{M}}:=\bm{M}+\bm{E}
, where the symmetric matrix
\bm{M}
has eigenvectors and eigenvalues
\bm{u}_{j}
and
\lambda_{j}
respectively.
Suppose
\lambda_{j}
is unique. Then the following expansion is valid always:

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\lambda_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}.

Proof.

See
Section

A.1
.
∎

The particular form of the first-order error decomposition was motivated by the proofs of
Koltchinskii and Lounici 2016
who derive their results using holomorphic functional calculus. In the setting considered herein the complex-analytic techniques of
Koltchinskii and Lounici 2016
do not apply as they implicitly require that
\Delta_{j}\gtrsim\|\bm{E}\|
. In addition, a similar first-order decomposition was used as an intermediate step in
Jirak and Wahl 2023
, though their focus is on providing perturbation bounds and limit theorems in non-Gaussian covariance models.

We will also use extensively the following matrix analysis identity that allows us to quantify the influence of the null space of a rank
r
matrix
\bm{M}
on the
j
’th estimated eigenvector
\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}
.

Theorem 8.2
.

Let
\bm{\widehat{M}}=\bm{M}+\bm{E}
, and let
\{\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j},\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\}
denote the eigenvectors and eigenvalues of
\bm{\widehat{M}}
and
\bm{u}_{j},\lambda_{j}
denote the eigenvectors and eigenvalues of
\bm{M}
. Suppose
\bm{M}
is rank
r
with orthogonal complement
\bm{U}_{\perp}
, such that
\bm{M}=\bm{U}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{U}^{\top}
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle\equiv\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\bm{U}^{-k}(\bm{U}^{-k})^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1},

provided the inverse is defined. Here
\bm{U}^{-k}
corresponds to the matrix
\bm{U}
with its
k
’th column removed.

Proof.

See
Section

A.2
.
∎

Theorem

8.2
allows for a decoupling of randomness as well as aids in identifying the appropriate eigenvalue bias term. As an immediate corollary to this result, we see that whenever
|\lambda_{j}|>3\|\bm{E}\|
it holds that
\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}
is invertible because

\displaystyle|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|\geq|\lambda_{j}|-\|\bm{E}\|>\frac{2|\lambda_{j}|}{3}>2\|\bm{E}\|\geq 2\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}\|,

which also implies that
|\lambda_{\min}\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)|\geq\frac{|\lambda_{j}|}{3}
.
As a consequence,

\displaystyle\|\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}\|\|\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\|\bm{E}\|}{|\lambda_{j}|}.

This result can be interpreted as a variant of the Davis-Kahan Theorem demonstrating the error along the
j
’th subspace scales with
|\lambda_{j}|^{-1}
.

8.2
Analysis for Matrix Denoising

Before embarking on the proof we first state several preliminary facts that are standard from nonasymptotic random matrix theory. We will use these facts repeatedly in our proofs. Without loss of generality we assume that
\lambda_{j}
is positive; otherwise repeat the analysis with
-\bm{\widehat{S}}
and
-\bm{S}
.

Fact 1
.

It holds that
\|\bm{N}\|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{n}
with probability at least
1-\exp(-cn)
. See, for example
Vershynin 2018
.

Fact 2
.

It holds that
\|\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}\|\lesssim\sigma\big(\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\big)
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. This can be proven via standard
\varepsilon
-net arguments, or one can appeal directly to the fact that
\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}
is a symmetric
r\times r
Gaussian Wigner matrix.

Fact 3
.

By virtue of
1
and Weyl’s inequality, it holds that
4|\lambda_{j}|/3+C\sigma\sqrt{n}\geq|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|\geq|\lambda_{j}|-C\sigma\sqrt{n}\geq 2|\lambda_{j}|/3
for all
j\leq r
and
|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{n}
for all
j\geq r+1
. Therefore, on the event
\|\bm{N}\|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{n}
, it holds that
|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|\in\big[2|\lambda_{j}|/3,4|\lambda_{j}|/3\big]
.

Fact 4
.

By virtue of
3
, it holds that
|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|\geq 2|\lambda_{\min}|/3\geq\|\bm{N}\|\geq\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}\|
, and hence
\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}
is invertible for all
|\lambda|\geq 2\lambda_{\min}/3
. Specifically,
\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}
is invertible on the event
\|\bm{N}\|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{n}
. In addition,
\|\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}\|\lesssim\lambda_{j}^{-1}
.

With these preliminary results out of the way we are now prepared to start the proof of
Theorems

3.1
and
3.2
and
Theorem

4.1
. The first part of our proof will be to characterize both the bias
\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}-1
as well as the leading eigenvalues, both of which will be used in the subsequent analysis. These steps rely heavily on the preliminary analysis in
Li et al. 2022
.

Throughout our proofs of our distributional theory results we assume that
\lambda_{1}
through
\lambda_{r}
are unique. If not, the extensions are only more notationally cumbersome and not significantly different.

8.2.1
Step 1: Characterizing the Bias

Theorem

8.1
yields an expansion for
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}
about
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}
, which contributes to the bias
\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-1
(recall that we assume that
\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\geq 0
). Therefore, the following lemma describes the bias.

Lemma 8.1

(Bias Characterization - Matrix Denoising)
.

Instate the conditions in
Theorem

3.1
. Define

\displaystyle b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}:

\displaystyle=\sum_{k>r}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{k})^{2}}.

Then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
it holds that

\displaystyle|1-(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j})^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log(n)}{\Delta_{j}^{2}};

\displaystyle\big|1-\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log(n)}{\Delta_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n\log(n)}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}.

Proof.

See
Section

B.1
.
∎

Lemma

8.1
also implies that:

•

The term
\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\approx\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}
up to some small error term.

•

The bias satisfies
b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}
(by Facts
1
and
4
).

8.2.2
Step 2: Eigenvalue Concentration

While
Theorem

8.1
is useful for determining the first-order error, it is often not quite enough to prove that the remaining two terms are sufficiently small order. In order to further analyze these residuals, we will introduce an additional “centering” term associated to the eigenvalue bias. Therefore, the following lemma characterizes this eigenvalue bias as well as its empirical approximation.

Lemma 8.2

(Eigenvalue Concentration - Matrix Denoising)
.

Instate the conditions of
Theorem

3.1
. Define

\displaystyle\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}):=\sigma^{2}{\sf{Tr}}\bigg(\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}\bigg);\qquad\widehat{\gamma}^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}):=\sum_{k>r}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{k}}.

Then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
it holds that

\displaystyle|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}-\lambda_{j}-\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\big(\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\big):=\delta^{{\sf MD}}.

Furthermore, with this same probability it holds that

\displaystyle|\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})-\widehat{\gamma}^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}r}{\lambda_{j}}.

Proof.

See
Section

B.2
.
∎

In words,
Lemma

8.2
demonstrates that:

•

The empirical eigenvalues
\widehat{\lambda}_{j}
concentrate around
\lambda_{j}+\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})
with error of order
\sigma(\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{\log(n)})
.

•

Moreover, when
\sigma^{2}
is known,
\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})
can be estimated directly from data via
\widehat{\gamma}^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})
with error at most
O\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{2}r}{\lambda_{j}}\bigg)
.

8.2.3
Step 3: Finding the Leading-Order Expansion

Now that we have characterized the bias
1-\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}
and the empirical eigenvalues, we are prepared to obtain the leading-order expansion for the plugin estimator
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}
about
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}
. Invoke
Theorem

8.1
(with
\bm{M}=\bm{S}
and
\bm{E}=\bm{N}
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}+\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\lambda_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j,k\leq r}\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}-\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}+\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}}{\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k\neq j,k\leq r}\frac{\lambda_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}+\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}+\frac{\lambda_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}}{\lambda_{j}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{1}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{2}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{3}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{4},

where we have subtracted
\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}
from the term containing
(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}
and added it to the term containing
\lambda_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}
for
k\leq r
. To understand the intuition behind adding and subtracting this quantity, observe that by
Theorem

8.2
,

\displaystyle(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}+\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}+\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}}_{\text{approximately mean-zero}}+\underbrace{\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}^{-k}(\bm{U}^{-k})^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}}_{\text{approximately mean-zero}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}}_{\text{approximately mean $\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})$}}.

The first quantity can be shown to be sufficiently small using the fact that
\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}
is an
r
-dimensional random vector. Similarly, the second term consists of products containing
(\bm{U}^{-k})^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}
,
\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}
, and
\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}
, all of which are mutually independent by virtue of the rotational invariance of the Gaussian distribution. However, the remaining term
\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}
is
not
approximately mean-zero, and in fact can be shown to concentrate about
\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})
, which is what motivates adding and subtracting this quantity.

The remainder of the argument requires demonstrating that
\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{1}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{2}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{3}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{4}=o(s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}})
. The following lemma accomplishes this. The proof of this lemma uses
Theorem

8.2
and

Lemmas

8.2
and
8.1
extensively.

Lemma 8.3
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

3.1
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-9})
it holds that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{1}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{2}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{3}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{4}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\frac{\sigma r\sqrt{n\log(n)}}{\lambda_{\min}}+\frac{\sigma r^{3/2}\log(n)}{\Delta_{j}}\bigg)s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}

\displaystyle={\sf ErrMD}\times s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}.

Proof.

See
Section

B.3
.
∎

Here we slightly abuse notation by letting
{\sf ErrMD}
absorb the implicit constants.

8.2.4
Step 4: Completing the Proofs of Theorems
3.1
and
3.2

We have shown thus far that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-9}),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\bigg)=\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}+{\sf ErrMD}

In addition, it is straightforward to observe that the leading-order term
\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}
is Gaussian with variance
(s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}})^{2}
, as
\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}
is independent from
\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{l}
for
l\neq k
by rotational invariance. As a consequence, letting
\Phi(\cdot)
denote the CDF of the Gaussian distribution, it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}

\displaystyle\big(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\big)\leq z\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bigg(\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{1}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{2}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{3}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf MD}}_{4}\bigg)\leq z\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bigg(\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}\bigg)\leq z\pm{\sf ErrMD}\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|+O(n^{-9})

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\Phi(z\pm{\sf ErrMD})-\Phi(z)\bigg|+O(n^{-9})

\displaystyle\lesssim{\sf ErrMD}+n^{-9},

where the final line follows from the Lipschitz property of
\Phi
, where
\Phi(z\pm x)
is interpreted as applying the result to both
\Phi(z+x)
and
\Phi(z-x)
separately. This completes the proof of
Theorem

3.1
.

We now prove
Theorem

3.2
.
By
Lemma

8.1
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
it holds that

\displaystyle|\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}-1|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log(n)}{\Delta_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n\log(n)}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}={\sf ErrBiasMD},

where again we have absorbed implicit constants. As a result,

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{

\displaystyle\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bigg)\leq z\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\big(\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-1\big)\bigg)\leq z\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bigg)\leq z\pm\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}{\sf ErrBiasMD}\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+O(n^{-9})

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\bigg)\leq\frac{z}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}\pm\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}{\sf ErrBiasMD}}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|+O(n^{-9})

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\bigg)\leq\frac{z}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}\pm\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}{\sf ErrBiasMD}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad-\Phi\bigg(\frac{z}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}\pm\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}{\sf ErrBiasMD}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\Phi(\frac{z}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}})-\Phi\bigg(\frac{z}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}\pm\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}{\sf ErrBiasMD}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}\bigg)\bigg|+O(n^{-9})

\displaystyle\lesssim{\sf ErrMD}+\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|{\sf ErrBiasMD}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}+n^{-9}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\sf ErrMD}+\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|{\sf ErrBiasMD}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}+n^{-9},

where we have applied
Theorem

3.1
in the penultimate line. This completes the proof of
Theorem

3.2
.

8.2.5
Step 5: Variance Approximation and Proof of Theorem
4.1

In order to prove
Theorem

4.1
we need to demonstrate that the estimated variance

\displaystyle(\widehat{s}_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}})^{2}:=2\widehat{\sigma}^{2}\sum_{k\leq r,k\neq j}\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a})^{2}(1+\widehat{b_{k}^{{\sf MD}}})}{(\widecheck{\lambda}_{k}-\widecheck{\lambda}_{j})^{2}}+2\frac{\widehat{\sigma}^{2}\|\bm{\widehat{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}\|^{2}}{\widecheck{\lambda}_{j}^{2}}

yields a strong estimate of
(s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}})^{2}
. This analysis requires two steps. First we study the approximated bias
\widehat{b_{k}^{{\sf MD}}}
.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
k\in[r]
be fixed. Under the conditions of
Theorem

4.1
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{k}^{{\sf MD}}}}-\sqrt{1+b_{k}^{{\sf MD}}}\bigg|\lesssim\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}.

Proof.

See
Section

B.4
.
∎

As a consequence,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}-\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}.

Furthermore, we note that the analysis leading up to the proof of
Theorem

3.2
implies that

\displaystyle\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\big|\lesssim\big|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\big|+\sqrt{\log(n)}s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}+{\sf ErrMD}\times s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}\lesssim|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|+\sqrt{\log(n)}s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-8})
, provided that
{\sf ErrMD}\lesssim\sqrt{\log(n)}
, which holds by the assumptions in
Theorem

4.1
, though we will verify this directly in
Lemma

8.6
. Therefore, with this same probability,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}

\displaystyle-\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}.

(10)

Next we study the approximated variance
(\widehat{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}})^{2}
, which is accomplished through the following lemma.

Lemma 8.5
.

Instate the conditions in
Theorem

4.1
. If
\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}\|<\|\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{a}\|
, then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-8})
it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\widehat{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}-1\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\sqrt{\frac{r}{n}}+\max_{k\neq j}\frac{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},k}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\frac{\sigma\sqrt{r\log(n)}}{\Delta_{j}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log(n)}{\Delta_{\min}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\kappa\sqrt{rn\log(n)}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sigma\sqrt{n}}{\lambda_{\min}}\kappa^{2}\max_{k\neq j,k\leq r}\frac{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j})^{2}}{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k})^{2}}.

If instead
\|\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{a}\|\leq\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}\|
, then with this same probability,

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}-1\bigg|\lesssim\kappa\sqrt{\frac{r}{n}}+\max_{k\neq j}\frac{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},k}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\frac{\sigma\sqrt{r\log(n)}}{\Delta_{j}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log(n)}{\Delta_{\min}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\kappa\sqrt{rn\log(n)}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma\sqrt{n}}{\lambda_{\min}}.

Proof.

See
Section

B.5
.
∎

Let the error in
Lemma

8.5
be denoted as
{\sf ErrCIMD}
. As a consequence of this lemma, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-8})
,

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{\widehat{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}}-\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}-\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg(1-\frac{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}{\widehat{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}}\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\frac{\kappa\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg(\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\frac{\kappa\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\bigg){\sf ErrCIMD}

\displaystyle\qquad+{\sf ErrCIMD}\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\frac{\kappa\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+{\sf ErrCIMD}\sqrt{\log(n)},

(11)

as long as
{\sf ErrCIMD}=o(1)
, which we verify at the end of the proof. Here we have used the fact that the analysis leading to
Theorem

3.2
demonstrates that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|\lesssim\sqrt{\log(n)}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-8})
, as long as each of the quantities in
Theorem

3.2
are
o(1)
, which again we verify at the end of the proof.

We are now prepared to prove
Theorem

4.1
. We have that

\displaystyle\Bigg|

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{\widehat{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}}\leq z\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\leq z

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg(\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{\widehat{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}}-\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg)\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\leq z\pm\bigg(\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\frac{\kappa\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+{\sf ErrCIMD}\sqrt{\log(n)}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\Bigg|+O(n^{-8})

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\leq z

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\pm\bigg(\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\frac{\kappa\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+{\sf ErrCIMD}\sqrt{\log(n)}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad-\Phi\bigg\{z\pm\bigg(\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\frac{\kappa\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+{\sf ErrCIMD}\sqrt{\log(n)}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg\}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg|\Phi\bigg(z\pm\bigg(\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\frac{\kappa\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+{\sf ErrCIMD}\sqrt{\log(n)}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg)-\Phi(z)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+O(n^{-8})

\displaystyle\lesssim{\sf ErrMD}+\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|{\sf ErrBiasMD}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}+\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\log(n)}\frac{\kappa\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}+{\sf ErrCIMD}\sqrt{\log(n)}+n^{-8},

where in the final line we have applied both
Theorem

3.2
and the Lipschitz property of
\Phi(\cdot)
. The proof can be completed provided we can show that each term on the right hand side above is
o(1)
under the conditions of
Theorem

4.1
. To avoid tedious algebra, this argument is provided in the following lemma.

Lemma 8.6
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

4.1
it holds that

\displaystyle\max\bigg\{{\sf ErrMD},\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|{\sf ErrBiasMD}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf MD}}}

\displaystyle,\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf MD}}_{\bm{a},j}}\kappa\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}},

\displaystyle\qquad\sqrt{\log(n)}\frac{\kappa\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rn}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}},{\sf ErrCIMD}\sqrt{\log(n)}\bigg\}=o(1).

Proof.

See
Section

B.6
.
∎

The proof is therefore concluded by taking
\pm z=\Phi^{-1}(1-\alpha/2)
.

8.3
Analysis for PCA

In this section we detail our analysis for the PCA model in
Section

3.2
; namely,
Theorems

3.3
and
3.4
. First will define some notation that we will use throughout this section and in associated proofs. Let
\bm{\widetilde{\Lambda}}=\bm{\Lambda}+\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{r}
, where
\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}=\lambda_{k}+\sigma^{2}
for
k\leq r
and denote
\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}=\sigma^{2}
for
k\geq r+1
. In this manner the eigenvalues of
\bm{\Sigma}
are simply
\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
. We also observe that we can write
\bm{X}=\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{Y}
, where
\bm{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times n}
is a matrix of independent
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
random variables. Throughout our proofs we assume that
\lambda_{1}
through
\lambda_{r}
are unique. The extensions to eigenvalue multiplicity are more cumbersome but not materially different.

We start with the following fact.

Fact 5
.

By (a slight modification of) Lemma 7 of
Li et al. 2022
, with probability at least
1-O((n\vee p)^{-10})
it holds that

\displaystyle\|\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{\max}\sqrt{\frac{r\log(n\vee p)}{n}}+\sqrt{(\lambda_{\max}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}\frac{p}{n}}\log(n\vee p)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sigma^{2}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}+\frac{p}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee p)}{n}}\bigg)

(12)

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}.

Remark 8.1

(Relationship to the noise assumption (
4
))
.

Our noise assumption (
4
) implies that
\lambda_{\min}\gg\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}
. To see this, we note that
r\lesssim\frac{n}{\kappa^{2}\log^{4}(n\vee p)}
implies that
\lambda_{\min}\geq\lambda_{\max}\sqrt{\frac{r}{n}}\log^{2}(n\vee p)
, which implies that the first quantity in (
12
) is smaller than
\lambda_{\min}/\log(n\vee p)
. In addition, for the second quantity in (
12
), we note that (
4
) implies that

\displaystyle\sqrt{(\lambda_{\max}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}\frac{p}{n}}\log(n\vee p)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}\frac{p}{n}}\log(n\vee p)+\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\log(n\vee p)\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{\min}}{\log(n\vee p)}.

Finally, for the third quantity in (
12
), it holds that

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}+\frac{p}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee p)}{n}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}\sigma^{2}\frac{p}{n}&n\leq p\\
\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p\log(n\vee p)}{n}}&n>p\end{cases}\qquad\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{\min}}{\log(n\vee p)}.

The next lemma provides an analogue of
2
for the PCA setting.

Lemma 8.7
.

Instate the conditions of
Theorem

3.3
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{U}\|\lesssim\big(\lambda_{\max}+\sigma^{2}\big)\sqrt{\frac{r\log(n\vee p)}{n}}.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma})\bm{U}\|

\displaystyle=\big\|\bm{\widetilde{\Lambda}}^{1/2}\bm{U}^{\top}\bigg(\frac{\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{Y}}{n}-\bm{I}_{p}\bigg)\bm{U}\bm{\widetilde{\Lambda}}^{1/2}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq(\lambda_{\max}+\sigma^{2})\big\|\bm{U}^{\top}\bigg(\frac{\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{Y}}{n}-\bm{I}_{p}\bigg)\bm{U}\big\|.

Note that
\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{Y}
is a
r\times n
dimensional standard Gaussian matrix. The result then follows from standard concentration inequalities for covariance matrices (e.g., Theorem 6.5 of
Wainwright 2019
) together with the assumption that
r\lesssim n
.
∎

Fact 6
.

By Weyl’s inequality and the assumption
\lambda_{\min}\gg\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}
, it holds that
\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\in[2(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})/3,4(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})/3]
on the event in
5
.

Fact 7
.

By
6
together with our noise assumption
\lambda_{\min}\gg\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}
, it holds that
\lambda\bm{I}_{p-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}\bm{U}_{\perp}
is invertible for any
\lambda\geq 2(\lambda_{\min}+\sigma^{2})/3
. Specifically,
\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{p-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}\bm{U}_{\perp}
is invertible on the event
\|\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\|\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}
. In addition,
\bigg\|\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{p-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}\bigg\|\lesssim\lambda_{j}^{-1}
, which can be seen since a similar argument to
Lemma

8.7
shows that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}\bm{U}_{\perp}\|\leq\|\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{p-r}\|+\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}\bm{U}_{\perp}-\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{p-r}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}+\sigma^{2}\bigg(\frac{p}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee p)}{n}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\ll\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2},

where the final inequality holds from the noise assumption (
4
). This argument demonstrates implies that
\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\geq 2[\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2}]\gg\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}\bm{U}_{\perp}\|
.

With these facts now stated we are able to embark on the proof of
Theorem

3.3
. As in the proofs for matrix denoising we will first characterize the bias term
\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-1
and study the eigenvalues of
\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}
, after which we will complete the proof.

8.3.1
Step 1: Characterizing the Bias

The following lemma studies the bias term
\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}
as well as provides the fidelity of the bias correction term
b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}
.

Lemma 8.8
.

Instate the conditions of
Theorem

3.3
, and define
b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}
as in
Theorem

3.4
. Then with probability at least
1-O((n\vee p)^{-10})
it holds that

\displaystyle|1-(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j})^{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\lambda_{\max}+\sigma^{2})(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})r\log(n\vee p)}{\Delta_{j}^{2}n}+\frac{(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}p\log^{2}(n\vee p)}{\lambda_{j}^{2}n}

\displaystyle=:(\mathcal{F}^{{\sf PCA}})^{2}.

(13)

In addition, with this same probability,

\displaystyle\big|1-\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\lambda_{\max}+\sigma^{2})(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})r\log(n\vee p)}{\Delta_{j}^{2}n}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sigma(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})^{1/2}}{\lambda_{j}\sqrt{n}}\Bigg(\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{r}\log^{2}(n\vee p)}{\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{r\kappa}\log^{2}(n\vee p)}{\lambda_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle={\sf ErrBiasPCA}.

Proof.

See
Section

C.1
.
∎

8.3.2
Step 2: Eigenvalue Concentration

We now provide similar guarantees to
Lemma

8.2
for the PCA setting.

Lemma 8.9
.

Instate the conditions of
Theorem

3.3
. Define

\displaystyle\gamma^{{\sf PCA}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}{\sf{Tr}}\Bigg(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}\bigg(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{p-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\frac{\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}}{n}\bm{U}_{\perp}\bigg)^{-1}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{X}\Bigg);

\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}^{{\sf PCA}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=r+1}^{\min\{p-r,n\}}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{k}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{k}}.

Then with probability at least
1-O((n\vee p)^{-10})
it holds that

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{\lambda}_{k}-\big(\lambda_{k}+\sigma^{2}\big)\big(1+\gamma^{{\sf PCA}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{k})\big)\big|\lesssim(\lambda_{\max}+\sigma^{2})\sqrt{\frac{r}{n}}\log(n\vee p)=:\delta^{{\sf PCA}}.

(14)

In addition, with this same probability it holds that

\displaystyle|\gamma^{{\sf PCA}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{j}n};

\displaystyle\bigg|\gamma^{{\sf PCA}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})-\widehat{\gamma}^{{\sf PCA}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}r}{n\lambda_{j}}\bigg(1+\frac{p}{n}\bigg).

Proof.

See
Section

C.2
.
∎

8.3.3
Step 3: Finding the Leading-Order Expansion

We are now prepared to establish the leading-order expansion for the difference
\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}
.
Invoke
Theorem

8.1
with
\bm{M}=\bm{\Sigma}
and
\bm{\widehat{M}}=\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}
to yield

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{u}_{k}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{u}_{k}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}.

Observe that
\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{u}_{k}=(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}=0
. Therefore, by rearranging and recognizing that
\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}=\lambda_{k}+\sigma^{2}
we arrive at

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{\top}

\displaystyle\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}+\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j,k\leq r}\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}+\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{U}_{\perp}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}}{\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k\neq j,k\leq r}\frac{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}+\frac{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}}{\lambda_{j}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j,k\leq r}\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{u}_{k}-\gamma^{{\sf PCA}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})(\lambda_{k}+\sigma^{2})\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{U}_{\perp}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}}{\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k\neq j,k\leq r}\frac{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}+\gamma^{{\sf PCA}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})(\lambda_{k}+\sigma^{2})}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}+\frac{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}}{\lambda_{j}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{1}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{2}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{3}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{4},

where in the penultimate line we have added and subtracted
\gamma^{{\sf PCA}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})(\lambda_{k}+\sigma^{2})\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}
for all
k\leq r
.
As in the case of matrix denoising, we will demonstrate first that the residuals
\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{1}
through
\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{4}
are sufficiently small relative to the standard deviation
s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}
, which is accomplished via the following lemma.

Lemma 8.10
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

3.3
with probability at least
1-O((n\vee p)^{-9})
it holds that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{1}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{2}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{3}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{4}|

\displaystyle\lesssim{\sf ErrPCA}\times s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}.

Proof.

See
Section

C.3
.
∎

Henceforth we let
{\sf ErrPCA}
absorb the implicit constants.

8.3.4
Step 4: Completing the Proofs of Theorems
3.3
and
3.4

We have shown thus far that with probability at least
1-O((n\vee p)^{-9})
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\bigg)=\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\bigg(\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}\bigg)+{\sf ErrPCA}.

We now study this leading-order term. Recalling that
\bm{X}_{i}=\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{Y}_{i}
, where
\bm{Y}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\bm{I}_{p})
, we can write
\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}=\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\frac{\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}}{n}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}
. Therefore, recognizing that the eigenvectors of
\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}
are the same as
\bm{\Sigma}
, the leading-order term further decomposes as

\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j,k\leq r}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bigg(\frac{\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}}{n}-\bm{I}_{p}\bigg)\bm{u}_{k}\frac{(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})^{1/2}(\lambda_{k}+\sigma^{2})^{1/2}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}

\displaystyle\quad+\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bigg(\frac{\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}}{n}-\bm{I}_{p}\bigg)\bm{U}_{\perp}\frac{\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})^{1/2}\sigma}{\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j,k\leq r}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bigg(\frac{\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}}{n}\bigg)\bm{u}_{k}\frac{(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})^{1/2}(\lambda_{k}+\sigma^{2})^{1/2}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}

\displaystyle\quad+\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bigg(\frac{\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}}{n}\bigg)\bm{U}_{\perp}\frac{\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})^{1/2}\sigma}{\lambda_{j}}

Since
\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{Y}
and
\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Y}
are independent for
j\neq k
, it is straightforward to demonstrate that the variance of this term is given by

\displaystyle(s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j,k\leq r}\frac{(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})(\lambda_{k}+\sigma^{2})(\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a})^{2}}{n(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k})^{2}}+\frac{(\lambda_{j}+\sigma^{2})\sigma^{2}\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}\|^{2}}{n\lambda_{j}^{2}}.

Unlike the matrix denoising setting, the leading-order term is not a Gaussian random variable, so we will demonstrate its approximate Gaussianity via the following lemma.

Lemma 8.11
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

3.3
it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}\leq z\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee p)}{n}}.

Proof.

See
Section

C.4
.
∎

Therefore, for any
z\in\mathbb{R}
it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\bigg)\leq z\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}\leq z-\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\bigg(\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{1}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{2}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{3}+\mathcal{R}^{{\sf PCA}}_{4}\bigg)\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}\leq z\pm{\sf ErrPCA}\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|+O((n\vee p)^{-9}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\big(\bm{\widehat{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}\big)\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}\leq z\pm{\sf ErrPCA}\bigg\}-\Phi(z\pm{\sf ErrPCA})\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+|\Phi(z)-\Phi(z\pm{\sf ErrPCA})|+O((n\vee p)^{-9})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee p)}{n}}+{\sf ErrPCA}+(n\vee p)^{-9}\lesssim{\sf ErrPCA}.

This completes the proof of
Theorem

3.3
.

We now prove
Theorem

3.4
. Let
{\sf ErrBiasPCA}
be as in
Lemma

8.8
, though absorbing constant factors. Observe that

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bigg)\leq z\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\big(\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-1\big)\bigg)\leq z\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\bigg)\leq\frac{z}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}\pm\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}{\sf ErrBiasPCA}}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+O((n\vee p)^{-9})

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\bigg(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\bigg)\leq\frac{z}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}\pm\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}{\sf ErrBiasPCA}}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad-\Phi\bigg(\frac{z}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}\pm\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}{\sf ErrBiasPCA}}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\Phi\bigg(\frac{z}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}\pm\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}{\sf ErrBiasPCA}}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}\bigg)-\Phi\bigg(\frac{z}{\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}\bigg)\bigg|+O((n\vee p)^{-9})

\displaystyle\lesssim{\sf ErrPCA}+\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|{\sf ErrBiasPCA}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}.

This completes the proof of
Theorem

3.4
.

8.3.5
Step 6: Variance Approximation and Proof of Theorem
4.2

Similar to the proof of
Theorem

4.1
we first study the estimated bias-correction value
\widehat{b_{k}^{{\sf PCA}}}
.

Lemma 8.12
.

If
p>n
, with probability at least
1-O((n\vee p)^{-10})
it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{k}^{{\sf PCA}}}}-\sqrt{1+b_{k}^{{\sf PCA}}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{\min}n}\bigg(\sqrt{r}\kappa\bigg(\frac{1}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee p)}{np}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{r}}{p}\frac{\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}}{\lambda_{\min}}\bigg).

If
n\geq p
then
\widehat{b_{k}^{{\sf PCA}}}=b_{k}^{{\sf PCA}}
.

Proof.

See
Section

C.5
.
∎

In what follows, let

\displaystyle{\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}:=\begin{cases}\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{\min}n}\bigg(\sqrt{r}\kappa\bigg(\frac{1}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee p)}{np}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{r}}{p}\frac{\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}}{\lambda_{\min}}\bigg)&p>n;\\
0&p\leq n,\end{cases}

(15)

absorbing constants if necessary.
As a consequence of
Lemma

8.12
, with probability at least
1-O((n\vee p)^{-9})
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{k}^{{\sf PCA}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}-\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{k}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}{\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}.

Our previous analysis implies that

\displaystyle|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}|\lesssim|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|+\log(n\vee p)s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}+{\sf ErrPCA}\times s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}\lesssim|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|+\log(n\vee p)s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}},

with probability at least
1-O((n\vee p)^{-8})
, provided that
{\sf ErrPCA}\lesssim\log(n\vee p)
, which we verify in
Lemma

8.14
. Therefore, with this same probability,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{k}^{{\sf PCA}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}

\displaystyle-\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{k}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}{\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}+\log(n\vee p){\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}.

We next consider the approximated variance.
The following result establishes the proximity of the estimated variance
\widehat{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}
to
s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}
.

Lemma 8.13
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

4.2
, with probability at least
1-O((n\vee p)^{-8})
the following holds. If
\|\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{a}\|\leq\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}\|
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\widehat{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-1\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(1+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{j}}\bigg)\bigg(\kappa\sqrt{\frac{r}{n}}\log(n\vee p)+\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(1+\max_{k\neq j}\frac{s_{\bm{a},k}^{{\sf PCA}}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\bigg)\frac{(\lambda_{\max}+\sigma^{2})\sqrt{r}\log(n\vee p)}{\Delta_{j}\sqrt{n}}+\mathcal{E}_{b}+\frac{\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}}{\lambda_{\min}},

and if
\|\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{a}\|<\|\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{a}\|
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\widehat{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-1\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(1+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{j}}\bigg)\bigg(\kappa\sqrt{\frac{r}{n}}\log(n\vee p)+\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}\bigg)+\bigg(1+\max_{k\neq j}\frac{s_{\bm{a},k}^{{\sf PCA}}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}\bigg)\frac{(\lambda_{\max}+\sigma^{2})\sqrt{r}\log(n\vee p)}{\Delta_{j}\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{E}_{b}+\frac{\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}}{\lambda_{\min}}\kappa^{2}\max_{k\neq j,k\leq r}\frac{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j})^{2}}{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k})^{2}}.

Here the quantity
\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}
is defined in (
12
), and the quantities
\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{E}_{b}
are defined via

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\sqrt{r}\kappa\frac{\log^{4}(n\vee p)}{n}+\frac{\sqrt{r}}{p}\frac{\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}}{\lambda_{\min}}&n/\log^{4}(n\vee p)<p;\\
\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(p\vee n)}{n}}&n/\log^{4}(n\vee p)\geq p.\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{b}:

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{(\lambda_{\max}+\sigma^{2})^{2}r\log(n\vee p)}{\Delta_{\min}^{2}n}&\\
\qquad+\bigg(\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}\sqrt{n}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{r}\log^{2}(n\vee p)}{\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{r\kappa}\log^{2}(n\vee p)}{\lambda_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\bigg)&\\
\qquad+\frac{\sigma^{2}p}{\lambda_{\min}n}\bigg(\sqrt{r}\kappa\bigg(\frac{1}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee p)}{np}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{r}}{p}\frac{\mathcal{E}^{{\sf PCA}}}{\lambda_{\min}}\bigg)\Bigg)&p>n;\\
\frac{(\lambda_{\max}+\sigma^{2})^{2}r\log(n\vee p)}{\Delta_{\min}^{2}n}&\\
\qquad+\bigg(\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}\sqrt{n}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{\sigma\kappa\sqrt{r}\log^{2}(n\vee p)}{\lambda_{\min}^{1/2}}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{r\kappa}\log^{2}(n\vee p)}{\lambda_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\bigg)&p\leq n.\end{cases}

Proof.

See
Section

C.6
.
∎

Let the error from
Lemma

8.13
be denoted
{\sf ErrCIPCA}
. Then with probability at least
1-O((n\vee p)^{-8})
,

\displaystyle\bigg|

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{\widehat{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}}-\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}-\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg(1-\frac{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}{\widehat{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}}\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}{\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}+\log(n\vee p){\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}

\displaystyle\quad+{\sf ErrCIPCA}\bigg(\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}{\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}+\log(n\vee p){\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+{\sf ErrCIPCA}\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}{\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}+\log(n\vee p){\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}+{\sf ErrCIPCA}\log(n\vee p),

(16)

as long as
{\sf ErrCIPCA}=o(1)
, which we will verify at the end of the proof. Here we have used the fact that the analysis leading to
Theorem

3.4
implies that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg|\lesssim\log(n\vee p)

with probability at least
1-O((n\vee p)^{-8})
as long as each of the quantities in
Theorem

3.4
are
o(1)
, which we will again verify in
Lemma

8.14
.

With these results stated we are prepared to prove
Theorem

4.2
. For a given
z\in\mathbb{R}
, by a similar analysis to the proof of
Theorem

4.1
,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{\widehat{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}}\leq z\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}\leq z

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\bigg(\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+\widehat{b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{\widehat{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}}-\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}\bigg)\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}\leq z\pm

\displaystyle\quad\bigg(\bigg[\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}+\log(n\vee p)\bigg]{\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}+{\sf ErrCIPCA}\log(n\vee p)\bigg)\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad+O((n\vee p)^{-8})

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf PCA}}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}\leq z\pm

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg(\bigg[\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}+\log(n\vee p)\bigg]{\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}+{\sf ErrCIPCA}\log(n\vee p)\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad-\Phi\bigg\{z\pm\bigg(\bigg[\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}+\log(n\vee p)\bigg]{\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}+{\sf ErrCIPCA}\log(n\vee p)\bigg)\bigg\}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg|\Phi\bigg\{z\pm\bigg(\bigg[\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}+\log(n\vee p)\bigg]{\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}+{\sf ErrCIPCA}\log(n\vee p)\bigg)\bigg\}-\Phi(z)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+O((n\vee p)^{-8})

\displaystyle\lesssim{\sf ErrPCA}+\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|{\sf ErrBiasPCA}}{s_{\bm{a},j}^{{\sf PCA}}}+\bigg[\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}+\log(n\vee p)\bigg]{\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}

\displaystyle\quad+{\sf ErrCIPCA}\log(n\vee p),

We can therefore complete the proof if we can demonstrate that the conditions in
Theorem

4.2
imply that each of these terms on the right hand side are
o(1)
, which is accomplished via the following lemma.

Lemma 8.14
.

Under the conditions of
Theorem

4.2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\max\bigg\{{\sf ErrPCA},

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}{\sf ErrBiasPCA},\log(n\vee p){\sf ErrCIPCA},

\displaystyle\quad\bigg(\frac{|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}|}{s^{{\sf PCA}}_{\bm{a},j}}+\log(n\vee p)\bigg){\sf ErrBiasApproxPCA}\bigg\}=o(1).

Proof.

See
Section

C.7
.
∎

The proof of
Theorem

4.2
immediately follows by taking
\pm z=\Phi^{-1}(1-\alpha/2)
.

Appendix A
Proofs of Matrix Analysis Results

In this section we prove our main deterministic results.

A.1
Proof of Theorem
8.1

Proof.

First, note that by the eigenvector-eigenvalue equation, it holds that

\displaystyle(\lambda_{k}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j})\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}=-\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{u}_{k}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{j})\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}+(\lambda_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j})\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}

\displaystyle=-\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{u}_{k}.

Diving through by
(\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{j})
and rearranging yields

\displaystyle\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}+\frac{\lambda_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}

\displaystyle=\bm{a}^{\top}(\bm{I}-\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top})\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bigg(\frac{\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}+\frac{\lambda_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}+\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}+\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}_{j}-\lambda_{j}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}.

By rearranging we arrive at

\displaystyle\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\sum_{k\neq j}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\frac{\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}-\bm{u}_{j})^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{u}_{k}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}+\sum_{k\neq j}\frac{\lambda_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}}{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{k}}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}\bm{a}.

This completes the proof.
∎

A.2
Proof of Theorem
8.2

Proof.

The second line is immediate as
\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}=\bm{u}_{k}\bm{u}_{k}^{\top}+\bm{U}^{-k}(\bm{U}^{-k})^{\top}
. Therefore, we start with the observation

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}=\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}(\bm{M}+\bm{E})\bm{U}_{\perp}=\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}=\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}+\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp},

which holds since
\bm{M}\bm{U}_{\perp}=0
. By rearranging this reveals that

\displaystyle\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)

\displaystyle=\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}.

Right multiplying through by
\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{E}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}
gives the result.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs of Matrix Denoising Lemmas and Corollaries

In this section we prove all of our intermediate results from
Section

8.2
as well as
Corollaries

3.1
and
4.1
.

B.1
Proof of Lemma
8.1

Proof of
Lemma

8.1
.

The first assertion follows from the argument in
Li et al. 2022
, where we note that our assumptions that
\lambda_{\min}\geq C_{0}\sigma\sqrt{nr}
is slightly stronger (by a factor of
\sqrt{r}
) than their assumption on
\lambda_{\min}^{\star}
, and our assumption on
\Delta_{j}
is also slightly stronger than their assumption on
\Delta_{j}
. Explicitly, they demonstrate that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})

one has

\displaystyle 1-(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j})^{2}

\displaystyle=1-\cos^{2}\theta\asymp\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}+O\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log(n)}{\Delta_{j}^{2}}\bigg)

Consequently,

\displaystyle|1-(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j})^{2}|\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}n}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log(n)}{\Delta_{j}^{2}}.

In addition, by defining
b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}
via

\displaystyle b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}:=\sum_{r<k\leq n}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}-\widehat{\lambda}_{k})^{2}},

then with this same probability one has

\displaystyle|(1+{b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}})(\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}^{\top}\bm{u}_{j})^{2}-1|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log(n)}{\Delta_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n\log(n)}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}

and hence

\displaystyle\big|1-\sqrt{1+b_{j}^{{\sf MD}}}\bm{u}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}r\log(n)}{\Delta_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\sqrt{n\log(n)}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}.

These results hold cumulatively with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.
∎

B.2
Proof of Lemma
8.2

In order to prove
Lemma

8.2
we will essentially replicate the proof of Theorem 7 of
Li et al. 2022
, only applying our slightly stronger assumption
\lambda_{\min}\geq C_{0}\sigma\sqrt{rn}
. Define

\displaystyle\bm{G}(\lambda):=\sigma^{2}{\sf{Tr}}\bigg(\big(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}\big)^{-1}\bigg)\bm{I}_{r},

(17)

and set

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{G}}(\lambda):=\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}\bigg(\lambda\bm{I}_{n-r}-\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}\bigg)^{-1}\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U},

(18)

where both quantities are understood as functions of
\lambda
with
\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}
considered fixed (i.e., conditional on
\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}
). In order to prove the result we will rely on the following lemma characterizing the concentration of
\|\bm{G}(\lambda)-\bm{\widetilde{G}}(\lambda)\|
.

Lemma B.1
.

In the setting of
Theorem

3.1
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda:|\lambda|\in[2|\lambda_{j}|/3,4|\lambda_{j}|/3]}\|\bm{G}(\lambda)-\bm{\widetilde{G}}(\lambda)\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}\sqrt{rn\log(n)}.

Proof of
Lemma

B.1
.

This result follows immediately from the proof of Lemma 1 of
Li et al. 2022
with the assumption
r\lesssim n/\log^{2}(n)
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

8.2
.

By
2
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
it holds that
\|\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}\|\lesssim\sigma\big(\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\big)
. Invoking Theorem 5 of
Li et al. 2022
it holds that

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{r}-\bm{\Lambda}-\bm{G}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})\big)\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}+\big(\bm{\widetilde{G}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})-\bm{G}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})\big)\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}.

Therefore, taking norms, it holds with probability
1-O(n^{-10})
that

\displaystyle\|\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{j}\bm{I}_{r}-\bm{\Lambda}-\bm{G}(\widehat{\lambda})\big)\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{\widehat{u}}_{j}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}\|+\sup_{\lambda:|\lambda|\in[2|\lambda_{j}|/3,4|\lambda_{j}|/3]}\big\|\big(\bm{\widetilde{G}}(\lambda)-\bm{G}(\lambda)\big)\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\big(\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\big)+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}\big(\sqrt{rn}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\big(\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{\log(n)}\big),

where we have the used that by
4
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-cn)
it holds that
\|\bm{N}\|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{n}
and hence on this event it holds that
|\widehat{\lambda}_{j}|\in[2|\lambda_{j}|/3,4|\lambda_{j}|/3]
. The final consequence follows from the assumption
\lambda_{\min}/\sigma\geq C_{0}\sqrt{rn}
for some sufficiently large constant
C_{0}
. The rest of the proof of the first assertion follows directly from the proof of Theorem 7 of
Li et al. 2022
.

We now study the approximation of
\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})
to
\widehat{\gamma}^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})
. Let
\lambda_{k}^{\perp}
denote the eigenvalues of
\bm{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\bm{N}\bm{U}_{\perp}
. Then

\displaystyle\gamma^{{\sf MD}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{j})
